# Canopi 2.1 decision ledger

Status: the user's decisions for Canopi 2.1 (epic canopi-f47t.52), from 2026-10-16. Rows are appended when a decision is taken; a row's id, date, decision and words are never rewritten, and only its supersedes and implemented-by columns gain text; a superseded or withdrawn row stays and names its successor.

`Q` rows are the user's. Engineering decisions taken under the user's mandate of 2026-10-17 carry an `E` or `M` id, so the user can overturn them; an `E` or `M` row never authorises a ratchet exception, a raised baseline or a change to a user-reviewed data file (ctr §12). Each row names the ADR rule, plan item (`plan`) or contract (`ctr`, the contracts file) that implements it; when the plan is deleted, its items live in code and tests.

| 2.1-M166 | 2026-10-20 | A shape helper takes either a const halo of at most `MAX_RECIPE_HALO` (windowed analyses) or a halo derived from the run's parameters under a named per-analysis ceiling (sunlight's shadow reach). How the area of interest is chosen is asked at the sunlight design check. Only target cells are traced and written to the run's output, and cancel checks fall at azimuth-bin boundaries. Latitude, longitude and the date set are passed in, never read from the window. Before the run starts, `admit` checks target plus halo against the 25 M-cell cap (`ExtentTooLarge`) and, on Web, against `EditionLimits`' measured Web whole-grid limit (`over_web_limit{limit}`). | 2.1-M167 | 2026-10-20 | One solar-position owner in the engine takes a UTC instant and WGS84 lon/lat and returns true azimuth and altitude; the timed overlay and every sunlight analysis read it, and no second solar-position code exists. Whether whitebox's shading core is used or copied (ADR 0002), its `solar_pos`, almanac, azimuth binning, fixed year and longitude-inferred UTC offset are not used. A day's samples span that date's whole sun-above-horizon arc at the site, so no zone or fixed offset sets a day boundary; the clock the user sees is a display conversion of the UTC instant, chosen at the sunlight design check. Hours of direct sun = sunlit samples x step, never a fraction x a day length. | 2.1-M168 | 2026-10-20 | Plant crowns form a canopy layer beside the surface model; they are not burnt into it. Each crown has a top and a spread at the chosen time, a crown base, a leaf state for the date and a transmissivity. The sun engine adapter casts the layer as a semi-transparent volume (Lindberg and Grimmond 2011, as in UMEP) by adapting a copied whitebox ray caster, because `time_in_daylight` and `shadow_image` take one opaque DEM; so "used or copied" (ADR 0002) is "copied" here. Crown tops stand on the ground model; where the site has a LiDAR surface model, its vegetation is not counted again under a planned crown. The lineage records the canopy layer's inputs and defaults. It holds the one crown-model function: a placed plant, its typed species facts, the growth choice the design check settles (mature size, growth phase or age) and the date give height, spread, crown base and leaf state. It returns `DesignCrownSet{at, crowns[{plantId, position, spreadM, heightM, baseM, leafHabit, spreadFrom, heightFrom}], missing[], digest}`; `missing` lists each plant with no usable size, shown by the form as a failure state; `digest` is the Design-snapshot input unit for either M130 option and the result's freshness key. | 2.1-Q262 | 2026-10-31 | CI is the authority on what merges and local runs are the fast path to it. One check list serves both: every Linux CI job calls `scripts/gate.py` (no inline check commands), so local green and CI red can only mean a platform or clean-environment problem. Local lanes are each complete for their scope: the commit lane adds the cheap source policy families (file size, test location, commit rules) to the typecheck and changed tests; the pre-push lane is the full Linux mirror, enforced by a pre-push hook; agents never push on the commit lane alone. CI keeps the platform, clean-machine, installer and mutation lanes and re-runs Linux as a backstop. Each CI failure a local check would have caught becomes a local check. The GitHub ruleset (Q222) makes the CI merge rule binding. | Agreed to all | reads Q222, M228, M286; gate path amended by Q267; hook wording amended by M348 | canopi-f47t.52.228.54 (Phase 1) |

| 2.1-Q330 | 2026-11-13 | How agentic steps run, to cut relaunches and late failures without dropping a check: (1) the owners file grants files per stream, not per chunk, and a stream also owns its own expected-fail markers, its snapshots and the generated files its change regenerates; only another stream's files are refused (a stream's chunks already run in order); (2) the commit lane runs the shared-contracts check (`check:types`) when its inputs change, so a stale binding or locale key fails at its commit; (3) build agents run at lower CPU priority than the main agent's gates and merges, and a step runs at most one Rust stream and one frontend stream at once; (4) from the next step, commit 0 adds a contract test for every module two streams change, run by both. | agreed (all four, recommended; contract tests from the next step) | changes 2.1-Q272's commit lane; process rows of docs/guides/agentic-delivery.md | plan Step 2.0.4 onward |

Accepted: a stale binding at its commit is worth seconds, a loud refusal beats a silent skip, and stream shells set CARGO_TARGET_DIR (in-wt.sh). This narrows M201's line that the commit lane runs only `cargo fmt --check` for Rust. | | amends 2.1-M201 | plan Step 2.0.4 (Q330 review) |

| 2.1-M505 | 2026-11-13 | Q331's hook in detail: anywhere it denies `--no-verify` on a git command that runs hooks (commit, push, merge, pull, rebase, cherry-pick, revert, am), `HUSKY=` set for a git command (prefix, `env` or an earlier `export`), a `core.hooksPath` override or config write, and a stash that touches the shared stack without naming its entry (`git stash`, `pop`, `clear`, `drop` with no entry); in the user's checkout it denies `reset --hard`, `checkout --`, `checkout .`, `checkout -f`, `restore` of the worktree, `clean -f`, `commit` and any stash but `list`, `show` or `create`. A `cd` inside a subshell stays inside it.
